Mathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Behrend

Behrend.boxdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Behrend, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $n$ and $d$ be natural numbers. Then $\operatorname{box}$ applied to $n$ and $d$ is a finite set of the set of functions from elements of $\mathrm{Fin}$ applied to $n$ to natural numbers defined as the product finite set of the function that maps $x$ to the range of $d$.
Formula
$n , d \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{box} (n , d):= \operatorname{piFinset} (\lambda x . \FinsetrangeMACRoU {d})\in \mathrm{Finset} (\mathrm{Fin} (n)\rightarrow \Natural)$
French
Soient $n$ et $d$ des nombres naturels. Alors $\operatorname{box}$ appliqué à $n$ et $d$ est un ensemble fini de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $\mathrm{Fin}$ appliqué à $n$ à des nombres naturels défini comme l'ensemble fini de produits de la fonction qui envoie $x$ à l'image de $d$.
Behrend.dValuedefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Behrend, function or constant
Wording
the value of N (from the name)
English
Let $N$ be a natural number. Then the value of $N$ is a natural number defined as the floor of the quotient of the exponentiation of $N$ to $\operatorname{inv}$ applied to the value of $N$ and $2$.
Formula
$N \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{dValue} (N):= \operatorname{floor} (\frac{ N ^ {\operatorname{inv} (\operatorname{nValue} (N))}}{2})\in \Natural$
French
Soit $N$ un nombre naturel. Alors la valeur de $N$ est un nombre naturel défini comme le plancher du quotient entre la puissance de $N$ en $\operatorname{inv}$ appliqué à la valeur de $N$ et $2$.
Behrend.mapdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Behrend, function or constant
Wording
the map of d (from the name)
English
Let $d$ be a natural number. Then the map of $d$ is an additive monoid homomorphism of the set of functions from elements of $\mathrm{Fin}$ applied to $n$ to natural numbers and $\Natural$.
Formula
$d \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{map} (d)\in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\mathrm{Fin} (n)\rightarrow \Natural , \Natural)$
French
Soit $d$ un nombre naturel. Alors l'application de $d$ est un homomorphisme de monoïdes additif de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $\mathrm{Fin}$ appliqué à $n$ à des nombres naturels et $\Natural$.
Behrend.nValuedefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Behrend, function or constant
Wording
the value of N (from the name)
English
Let $N$ be a natural number. Then the value of $N$ is a natural number defined as $\operatorname{ceil}$ applied to the square root of the logarithm of $N$.
Formula
$N \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{nValue} (N):= \operatorname{ceil} (\operatorname{sqrt} (\operatorname{log} (N)))\in \Natural$
French
Soit $N$ un nombre naturel. Alors la valeur de $N$ est un nombre naturel défini comme $\operatorname{ceil}$ appliqué à la racine carrée du logarithme de $N$.
Behrend.spheredefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Behrend, function or constant
Wording
the sphere of d and k (from the name)
English
Let $n$, $d$ and $k$ be natural numbers. Then the sphere of $d$ and $k$ is a finite set of the set of functions from elements of $\mathrm{Fin}$ applied to $n$ to natural numbers defined as $\FinsetFilter {x}{\mathrm{Eq} (\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAC}{i}{(x (i)^ {2})}, k)}{\operatorname{box} (n , d)}$.
Formula
$n , d , k \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{sphere} (n , d , k):= \FinsetFilter {x}{\mathrm{Eq} (\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAJ}{i}{(x (i)^ {2})}, k)}{\operatorname{box} (n , d)}\in \mathrm{Finset} (\mathrm{Fin} (n)\rightarrow \Natural)$
French
Soient $n$, $d$ et $k$ des nombres naturels. Alors la sphère de $d$ et $k$ est un ensemble fini de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $\mathrm{Fin}$ appliqué à $n$ à des nombres naturels défini comme $\FinsetFilter {x}{\mathrm{Eq} (\FinsetSum {\FinsetunivMACRoAC}{i}{(x (i)^ {2})}, k)}{\operatorname{box} (n , d)}$.

ThreeAPFreedefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.AP.Three.Defs, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $s$ be an element of $\wp x$. Then $\mathrm{ThreeAPFree}$ holds for $s$, if for all elements $a$ of $x$, if $a$ is an element of $s$, then for all elements $b$ of $x$, if $b$ is an element of $s$, then for all elements $c$ of $x$, if $c$ is an element of $s$, then if $\EqtMACRoH {a + c}{b + b}$, then $\EqtMACRoH {a}{b}$.
Formula
$s \in \wp x \vdash \mathrm{ThreeAPFree} (s):\Leftrightarrow \forall a \in x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{a}\rightarrow (\forall b \in x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{b}\rightarrow (\forall c \in x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{c}\rightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {a + c}{b + b}\rightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {a}{b}))$
French
Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp x$. Alors $\mathrm{ThreeAPFree}$ tient pour $s$ si pour tous les éléments $a$ de $x$, si $a$ est un élément de $s$, alors pour tous les éléments $b$ de $x$, si $b$ est un élément de $s$, alors pour tous les éléments $c$ de $x$, si $c$ est un élément de $s$, alors si $\EqtMACRoH {a + c}{b + b}$, alors $\EqtMACRoH {a}{b}$.

Finset.addConvolutiondefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Convolution, function or constant
Wording
the additive convolution of B and x2 (from the name)
English
Let $A$ and $B$ be finite sets of $G$. Let $x_{2}$ be an element of $G$. Then the additive convolution of $B$ and $x_{2}$ is a natural number defined as the cardinality of $\FinsetFilter {\mathrm{ab}}{(\EqtMACRoH {(\ProdfstMACRoZ {\mathrm{ab}}+ \ProdsndMACRoAA {\mathrm{ab}})}{x_{2}})}{\operatorname{sprod} (A , B)}$.
Formula
$A , B \in \mathrm{Finset} (G), x_{2} \in G \vdash \operatorname{addConvolution} (A , B , x_{2}):= \FinsetcardMACRoT {(\FinsetFilter {\mathrm{ab}}{(\EqtMACRoJ {(\ProdfstMACRoAG {\mathrm{ab}}+ \ProdsndMACRoAH {\mathrm{ab}})}{x_{2}})}{\operatorname{sprod} (A , B)})}\in \Natural$
French
Soient $A$ et $B$ des ensembles finis de $G$. Soit $x_{2}$ un élément de $G$. Alors la convolution additive de $B$ et $x_{2}$ est un nombre naturel défini comme la cardinalité de $\FinsetFilter {\mathrm{ab}}{(\EqtMACRoH {(\ProdfstMACRoZ {\mathrm{ab}}+ \ProdsndMACRoAA {\mathrm{ab}})}{x_{2}})}{\operatorname{sprod} (A , B)}$.

Mathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Corner.Defs

IsCornerstructureMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Corner.Defs, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $A$ be an element of $\wp \mathrm{Prod} (G , G)$. Let $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$ be elements of $G$. Then we can say that $\mathrm{IsCorner}$ holds for $A$, $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$.
Formula
$A \in \wp \mathrm{Prod} (G , G), x_{1} , x_{2} , x_{3} , x_{4} \in G \vdash \mathrm{IsCorner} (A , x_{1} , x_{2} , x_{3} , x_{4})\ \mathsf{prop}$
French
Soit $A$ un élément de $\wp \mathrm{Prod} (G , G)$. Soient $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ et $x_{4}$ des éléments de $G$. Alors nous pouvons dire que $\mathrm{IsCorner}$ tient pour $A$, $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ et $x_{4}$.
IsCornerFreedefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Corner.Defs, predicate
Wording
A is a corner free (from the name)
English
Let $A$ be an element of $\wp \mathrm{Prod} (G , G)$. Then $A$ is a corner free, if for all elements $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$ of $G$, if $\mathrm{IsCorner}$ holds for $A$, $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$, then $\EqtMACRoH {x_{1}}{x_{3}}$.
Formula
$A \in \wp \mathrm{Prod} (G , G)\vdash \mathrm{IsCornerFree} (A):\Leftrightarrow \forall x_{1} \in G . \forall x_{2} \in G . \forall x_{3} \in G . \forall x_{4} \in G . \mathrm{IsCorner} (A , x_{1} , x_{2} , x_{3} , x_{4})\rightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {x_{1}}{x_{3}}$
French
Soit $A$ un élément de $\wp \mathrm{Prod} (G , G)$. Alors $A$ est un libre de coins si pour tous les éléments $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ et $x_{4}$ de $G$, si $\mathrm{IsCorner}$ tient pour $A$, $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ et $x_{4}$, alors $\EqtMACRoH {x_{1}}{x_{3}}$.

cornersTheoremBounddefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Corner.Roth, function or constant
Wording
the corner theorem bound of x (from the name)
English
Let $x$ be a real number. Then the corner theorem bound of $x$ is a natural number defined as the sum of the floor of $\operatorname{inv}$ applied to $\operatorname{triangleRemovalBound} (\frac{ x}{9})\times 27$ and $1$.
Formula
$x \in \Real \vdash \mathrm{cornersTheoremBound} (x):= \operatorname{floor} (\operatorname{inv} (\operatorname{triangleRemovalBound} (\frac{ x}{9})\times 27))+ 1 \in \Natural$
French
Soit $x$ un nombre réel. Alors la borne de théorèmes de coins de $x$ est un nombre naturel défini comme la somme du plancher de $\operatorname{inv}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{triangleRemovalBound} (\frac{ x}{9})\times 27$ et de $1$.

Mathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.CovBySMul

CovBySMuldefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.CovBySMul, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $M$ be a set. Let $K$ be a real number. Let $A$ and $B$ be elements of $\wp X$. Then $\mathrm{CovBySMul}$ holds for $M$, $K$, $A$ and $B$, if there exists a finite set $F$ of $M$, such that $\FinsetcardMACRoM {F}\leq K$ and $A \leq \HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {F}{B}$.
Formula
$M \in \mathrm{Set} , K \in \Real , A , B \in \wp X \vdash \mathrm{CovBySMul} (M , K , A , B):\Leftrightarrow \exists F \in \mathrm{Finset} (M). \FinsetcardMACRoT {F}\leq K \wedge A \leq \HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {F}{B}$
French
Soit $M$ un ensemble. Soit $K$ un nombre réel. Soient $A$ et $B$ des éléments de $\wp X$. Alors $\mathrm{CovBySMul}$ tient pour $M$, $K$, $A$ et $B$ s'il existe un ensemble fini $F$ de $M$ tel que $\FinsetcardMACRoM {F}\leq K$ et $A \leq \HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {F}{B}$.
CovByVAdddefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.CovBySMul, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $M$ be a set. Let $K$ be a real number. Let $A$ and $B$ be elements of $\wp X$. Then $\mathrm{CovByVAdd}$ holds for $M$, $K$, $A$ and $B$, if there exists a finite set $F$ of $M$, such that $\FinsetcardMACRoM {F}\leq K$ and $A$ is less than or equal to the sum of $F$ and $B$.
Formula
$M \in \mathrm{Set} , K \in \Real , A , B \in \wp X \vdash \mathrm{CovByVAdd} (M , K , A , B):\Leftrightarrow \exists F \in \mathrm{Finset} (M). \FinsetcardMACRoT {F}\leq K \wedge A \leq \operatorname{hVAdd} (F , B)$
French
Soit $M$ un ensemble. Soit $K$ un nombre réel. Soient $A$ et $B$ des éléments de $\wp X$. Alors $\mathrm{CovByVAdd}$ tient pour $M$, $K$, $A$ et $B$ s'il existe un ensemble fini $F$ de $M$ tel que $\FinsetcardMACRoM {F}\leq K$ et $A$ soit inférieur ou égal à la somme de $F$ et $B$.

AddDissociateddefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Dissociation, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $s$ be an element of $\wp x$. Then $\mathrm{AddDissociated}$ holds for $s$, if the function that maps $x_{2}$ to $\FinsetSum {x_{2}}{x_{3}}{x_{3}}$ is injective on $\operatorname{ofPred}$ applied to the function that maps $t$ to $\mathrm{Leq} (t , s)$.
Formula

French
Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp x$. Alors $\mathrm{AddDissociated}$ tient pour $s$ si la fonction qui envoie $x_{2}$ à $\FinsetSum {x_{2}}{x_{3}}{x_{3}}$ est injective sur $\operatorname{ofPred}$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $t$ à $\mathrm{Leq} (t , s)$.
Finset.addSpandefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.Dissociation, function or constant
Wording
the span of s (from the docstring)
English
Let $s$ be a finite set of $x$. Then the span of $s$ is a finite set of $x$ defined as the forward image of the product finite set of the function that maps $\mathrm{\_a}$ to $\operatorname{insert}$ applied to $\operatorname{neg}$ applied to $1$ and $\operatorname{insert}$ applied to $0$ and $\operatorname{singleton}$ applied to $1$ under the function that maps $x_{4}$ to $\FinsetSum {s}{a}{(\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {x_{4} (a)}{a})}$.
Formula

French
Soit $s$ un ensemble fini de $x$. Alors l'envergure de $s$ est un ensemble fini de $x$ défini comme l'image avant de l'ensemble fini de produits de la fonction qui envoie $\mathrm{\_a}$ à $\operatorname{insert}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{neg}$ appliqué à $1$ et $\operatorname{insert}$ appliqué à $0$ et $\operatorname{singleton}$ appliqué à $1$ sous la fonction qui envoie $x_{4}$ à $\FinsetSum {s}{a}{(\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {x_{4} (a)}{a})}$.

Finset.addConstdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Additive.DoublingConst, function or constant
Wording
the doubling constant of A and B (from the docstring)
English
Let $A$ and $B$ be finite sets of $G$. Then the doubling constant of $A$ and $B$ is a nonnegative rational number defined as $\frac{ \FinsetcardMACRoM {(A + B)}}{\FinsetcardMACRoM {A}}$.
Formula
$A , B \in \mathrm{Finset} (G)\vdash \operatorname{addConst} (A , B):= \frac{ \FinsetcardMACRoT {(A + B)}}{\FinsetcardMACRoT {A}}\in \mathrm{NNRat}$
French
Soient $A$ et $B$ des ensembles finis de $G$. Alors la constante de doublements de $A$ et $B$ est un nombre rationnel positif défini comme $\frac{ \FinsetcardMACRoM {(A + B)}}{\FinsetcardMACRoM {A}}$.

Wording
the subset sum of A (from the name)
English
Let $A$ be a finite set of $M$. Then the subset sum of $A$ is a finite set of $M$ defined as the forward image of the powerset of $A$ under the function that maps $B$ to $\FinsetSum {B}{a}{\mathrm{id} (a)}$.

Mathlib.Combinatorics.Colex

Finset.Colex.IsInitSegdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Colex, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x_{2}$ be a finite set of the set of finite sets of $x$. Let $r$ be a natural number. Then $\operatorname{IsInitSeg}$ holds for $x_{2}$ and $r$, if $\operatorname{Sized}$ holds for $r$ and $x_{2}$ and for all finite sets $s$ of $x$, for all finite sets $t$ of $x$, if $s$ is an element of $x_{2}$, then if $\operatorname{toColex} (t)< \operatorname{toColex} (s)$ and $\FinsetcardMACRoM {t}= r$, then $t$ is an element of $x_{2}$.
Formula
$x_{2} \in \mathrm{Finset} (\mathrm{Finset} (x)), r \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{IsInitSeg} (x_{2} , r):\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Sized} (r , x_{2})\wedge (\forall s \in \mathrm{Finset} (x). \forall t \in \mathrm{Finset} (x). \MembershipmemMACRoAA {x_{2}}{s}\rightarrow \operatorname{toColex} (t)< \operatorname{toColex} (s)\wedge \FinsetcardMACRoT {t}= r \rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {x_{2}}{t})$
French
Soit $x_{2}$ un ensemble fini de l'ensemble d'ensembles finis de $x$. Soit $r$ un nombre naturel. Alors $\operatorname{IsInitSeg}$ tient pour $x_{2}$ et $r$ si $\operatorname{Sized}$ tient pour $r$ et $x_{2}$ et pour tous des ensembles finis $s$ de $x$, pour tous des ensembles finis $t$ de $x$, si $s$ est un élément de $x_{2}$, alors si $\operatorname{toColex} (t)< \operatorname{toColex} (s)$ et $\FinsetcardMACRoM {t}= r$, alors $t$ est un élément de $x_{2}$.

DyckWord.IsNesteddefMathlib.Combinatorics.Enumerative.DyckWord, predicate
Wording
p is nested (from the name)
English
Let $p$ be a Dyck word. Then $p$ is nested, if $p \neq 0$ and for all natural numbers $i$, if $0 < i$, then if $i$ is less than $\operatorname{length}$ applied to the underlying list of $p$, then the multiplicity of $\operatorname{D}$ in $\operatorname{take}$ applied to $i$ and the underlying list of $p$ is less than the multiplicity of $\operatorname{U}$ in $\operatorname{take}$ applied to $i$ and the underlying list of $p$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{DyckWord} \vdash \operatorname{IsNested} (p):\Leftrightarrow p \neq 0 \wedge (\forall i \in \Natural . 0 < i \rightarrow i < \operatorname{length} (\operatorname{toList} (p))\rightarrow \operatorname{count} (\operatorname{D} , \operatorname{take} (i , \operatorname{toList} (p)))< \operatorname{count} (\operatorname{U} , \operatorname{take} (i , \operatorname{toList} (p))))$
French
Soit $p$ un mot de Dyck. Alors $p$ est emboîté si $p \neq 0$ et pour tous des nombres naturels $i$, si $0 < i$, alors si $i$ est inférieur à $\operatorname{length}$ appliqué à la liste sous-jacente de $p$, alors la multiplicité de $\operatorname{D}$ dans $\operatorname{take}$ appliqué à $i$ et la liste sous-jacente de $p$ est inférieure à la multiplicité de $\operatorname{U}$ dans $\operatorname{take}$ appliqué à $i$ et la liste sous-jacente de $p$.

Graph.AdjdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Graph.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $G$ be a graph of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $x_{2}$ and $y$ be elements of $x$. Then $\operatorname{Adj}$ holds for $G$, $x_{2}$ and $y$, if there exists an element $e$ of $x_{1}$, such that $\operatorname{IsLink}$ holds for $G$, $e$, $x_{2}$ and $y$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Graph} (x , x_{1}), x_{2} , y \in x \vdash \operatorname{Adj} (G , x_{2} , y):\Leftrightarrow \exists e \in x_{1} . \operatorname{IsLink} (G , e , x_{2} , y)$
French

Graph.CompatibledefMathlib.Combinatorics.Graph.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $G$ and $H$ be graphs of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Then $\operatorname{Compatible}$ holds for $G$ and $H$, if for all elements $e$ of $x_{1}$, if $e$ is an element of the edge set of $G$, then if $e$ is an element of the edge set of $H$, then for all elements $x_{2}$ and $y$ of $x$, $\operatorname{IsLink}$ holds for $G$, $e$, $x_{2}$ and $y$, if and only if $\operatorname{IsLink}$ holds for $H$, $e$, $x_{2}$ and $y$.
Formula
$G , H \in \mathrm{Graph} (x , x_{1})\vdash \operatorname{Compatible} (G , H):\Leftrightarrow \forall e \in x_{1} . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{edgeSet} (G)}{e}\rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{edgeSet} (H)}{e}\rightarrow (\forall x_{2} \in x . \forall y \in x . \operatorname{IsLink} (G , e , x_{2} , y)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{IsLink} (H , e , x_{2} , y))$
French
Soient $G$ et $H$ des graphes de $x$ et $x_{1}$. Alors $\operatorname{Compatible}$ tient pour $G$ et $H$ si pour tous les éléments $e$ de $x_{1}$, si $e$ est un élément de l'ensemble d'arêtes de $G$, alors si $e$ est un élément de l'ensemble d'arêtes de $H$, alors pour tous les éléments $x_{2}$ et $y$ de $x$, $\operatorname{IsLink}$ tient pour $G$, $e$, $x_{2}$ et $y$ si et seulement si $\operatorname{IsLink}$ tient pour $H$, $e$, $x_{2}$ et $y$.
Graph.IncdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Graph.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $G$ be a graph of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $e$ be an element of $x_{1}$. Let $x_{2}$ be an element of $x$. Then $\operatorname{Inc}$ holds for $G$, $e$ and $x_{2}$, if there exists an element $y$ of $x$, such that $\operatorname{IsLink}$ holds for $G$, $e$, $x_{2}$ and $y$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Graph} (x , x_{1}), e \in x_{1} , x_{2} \in x \vdash \operatorname{Inc} (G , e , x_{2}):\Leftrightarrow \exists y \in x . \operatorname{IsLink} (G , e , x_{2} , y)$
French

Graph.IsLinkdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Graph.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a graph of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $x_{2}$ be an element of $x_{1}$. Let $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$ be elements of $x$. Then $\operatorname{IsLink}$ holds for $\mathrm{self}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$, if $\operatorname{proj1}$ holds for $\mathrm{self}$, $x_{2}$, $x_{3}$ and $x_{4}$.
Formula

Wording
no wording
English

Formula

Wording
G is an edge cut (from the docstring)
English
Let $G$ be a graph of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $F$ be an element of $\wp x_{1}$. Then $G$ is an edge cut, if there exists an element $S$ of $\wp x$, such that the edge cut of $G$ and $S$ is equal to $F$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Graph} (x , x_{1}), F \in \wp x_{1} \vdash \operatorname{IsEdgeCut} (G , F):\Leftrightarrow \exists S \in \wp x . \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{edgeCut} (G , S)}{F}$

Graph.edgeCutdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Graph.Connected.EdgeCut, function or constant
Wording
the edge cut of G and S (from the docstring)
English
Let $G$ be a graph of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $S$ be an element of $\wp x$. Then the edge cut of $G$ and $S$ is an element of $\wp x_{1}$ defined as $\operatorname{ofPred}$ applied to the function that maps $e$ to the proposition that there exist elements $u$ and $v$ of $x$, such that $\operatorname{IsLink}$ holds for $G$, $e$, $u$ and $v$, $u$ is an element of $S$ and $v$ is not an element of $S$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Graph} (x , x_{1}), S \in \wp x \vdash \operatorname{edgeCut} (G , S):= \operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda e . \exists u \in x . \exists v \in x . \operatorname{IsLink} (G , e , u , v)\wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {S}{u}\wedge \neg (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {S}{v})))\in \wp x_{1}$
French

Alors la coupure d'arêtes de $G$ et $S$ est un élément de $\wp x_{1}$ défini comme $\operatorname{ofPred}$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $e$ à la proposition qu'il existent des éléments $u$ et $v$ de $x$ tel que $\operatorname{IsLink}$ tienne pour $G$, $e$, $u$ et $v$, $u$ soit un élément de $S$ et $v$ ne soit pas un élément de $S$.

Hypergraph.AdjdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Hypergraph.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $H$ be a hypergraph of $x$. Let $x_{1}$ and $y$ be elements of $x$. Then $\operatorname{Adj}$ holds for $H$, $x_{1}$ and $y$, if there exists an element $e$ of $\wp x$, such that $e$ is an element of the edge set of $H$, $x_{1}$ is an element of $e$ and $y$ is an element of $e$.
Formula
$H \in \mathrm{Hypergraph} (x), x_{1} , y \in x \vdash \operatorname{Adj} (H , x_{1} , y):\Leftrightarrow \exists e \in \wp x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{edgeSet} (H)}{e}\wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {e}{x_{1}}\wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {e}{y}))$
French
Soit $H$ un hypergraphe de $x$. Soient $x_{1}$ et $y$ des éléments de $x$. Alors $\operatorname{Adj}$ tient pour $H$, $x_{1}$ et $y$ s'il existe un élément $e$ de $\wp x$ tel que $e$ soit un élément de l'ensemble d'arêtes de $H$, $x_{1}$ soit un élément de $e$ et $y$ soit un élément de $e$.
Hypergraph.EAdjdefMathlib.Combinatorics.Hypergraph.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $H$ be a hypergraph of $x$. Let $e$ and $f$ be elements of $\wp x$. Then $\operatorname{EAdj}$ holds for $H$, $e$ and $f$, if $e$ is an element of the edge set of $H$, $f$ is an element of the edge set of $H$ and there exists an element $x_{1}$ of $x$ such that $x_{1}$ is an element of $e$ and $x_{1}$ is an element of $f$.
Formula
$H \in \mathrm{Hypergraph} (x), e , f \in \wp x \vdash \operatorname{EAdj} (H , e , f):\Leftrightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{edgeSet} (H)}{e}\wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{edgeSet} (H)}{f}\wedge (\exists x_{1} \in x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {e}{x_{1}}\wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {f}{x_{1}})))$

Wording
P is a exchange property (from the name)
English
Let $P$ be a function from elements of $\wp x$ to propositions. Then $P$ is an exchange property, if for all elements $X$ and $Y$ of $\wp x$, if $P$ holds for $X$, then if $P$ holds for $Y$, then for all elements $a$ of $x$, if $\MembershipmemMACRoT {\operatorname{sdiff} (X , Y)}{a}$, then there exists an element $b$ of $x$, such that $\MembershipmemMACRoT {\operatorname{sdiff} (Y , X)}{b}$ and $P$ holds for $\operatorname{insert}$ applied to $b$ and $\operatorname{sdiff}$ applied to $X$ and $\operatorname{singleton}$ applied to $a$.
Formula
$P \in \wp x \rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} \vdash \operatorname{ExchangeProperty} (P):\Leftrightarrow \forall X \in \wp x . \forall Y \in \wp x . P (X)\rightarrow P (Y)\rightarrow (\forall a \in x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{sdiff} (X , Y)}{a}\rightarrow (\exists b \in x . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{sdiff} (Y , X)}{b}\wedge P (\operatorname{insert} (b , \operatorname{sdiff} (X , \operatorname{singleton} (a))))))$
French
generation took over the time limit
Matroid.ExistsMaximalSubsetPropertydefMathlib.Combinatorics.Matroid.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English

Then $\operatorname{ExistsMaximalSubsetProperty}$ holds for $P$ and $X$, if for all elements $I$ of $\wp x$, if $P$ holds for $I$, then if $I \leq X$, then there exists an element $J$ of $\wp x$, such that $I \leq J$ and $J$ is maximal with respect to the function that maps $K$ to the proposition that $P$ holds for $K$ and $K \leq X$.

Matroid.copydefMathlib.Combinatorics.Matroid.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the copy of IsBase and Indep (from the name)
English
Let $M$ be a matroid of $x$. Let $E$ be an element of $\wp x$. Let $\mathrm{IsBase}$ and $\mathrm{Indep}$ be functions from elements of $\wp x$ to propositions. Assume that $\EqtMACRoH {E}{\operatorname{E} (M)}$. Assume that for all elements $B$ of $\wp x$, $\mathrm{IsBase}$ holds for $B$, if and only if $\operatorname{IsBase}$ holds for $M$ and $B$. Assume that for all elements $I$ of $\wp x$, $\mathrm{Indep}$ holds for $I$, if and only if $M$ is independent to $I$. Then the copy of $\mathrm{IsBase}$ and $\mathrm{Indep}$ is a matroid of $x$.
Formula
$M \in \mathrm{Matroid} (x), E \in \wp x , \mathrm{IsBase} , \mathrm{Indep} \in \wp x \rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} , \EqtMACRoJ {E}{\operatorname{E} (M)}, \forall B \in \wp x . \mathrm{IsBase} (B)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{IsBase} (M , B), \forall I \in \wp x . \mathrm{Indep} (I)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{Indep} (M , I)\vdash \operatorname{copy} (M , E , \mathrm{IsBase} , \mathrm{Indep} , \mathrm{hE} , \mathrm{hB} , \mathrm{hI})\in \mathrm{Matroid} (x)$
French
Soit $M$ un matroïde de $x$. Soit $E$ un élément de $\wp x$. Soient $\mathrm{IsBase}$ et $\mathrm{Indep}$ des fonctions des éléments de $\wp x$ à des propositions. Supposons que $\EqtMACRoH {E}{\operatorname{E} (M)}$. Supposons que pour tous les éléments $B$ de $\wp x$, $\mathrm{IsBase}$ tient pour $B$ si et seulement si $\operatorname{IsBase}$ tient pour $M$ et $B$. Supposons que pour tous les éléments $I$ de $\wp x$, $\mathrm{Indep}$ tient pour $I$ si et seulement si $M$ est indépendant à $I$. Alors le copy de $\mathrm{IsBase}$ et $\mathrm{Indep}$ est un matroïde de $x$.

Then the independent matroid of $E$ and $\mathrm{Indep}$ is an independent matroid of $x$.

(\forall J \in \wp x . Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$. Assume that $\EqtMACRoH {X}{\mathrm{X'}}$. Assume that $\EqtMACRoH {Y}{\mathrm{Y'}}$. Then the homomorphism of $f$ is a homomorphism of $\mathrm{X'}$ and $\mathrm{Y'}$ defined as $\operatorname{ndrec}$ applied to $\operatorname{ndrec}$ applied to $f$ and $\mathrm{hY}$ and $\mathrm{hX}$.
Formula
$f \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y), \EqtMACRoJ {X}{\mathrm{X'}}, \EqtMACRoJ {Y}{\mathrm{Y'}}\vdash \operatorname{homOfEq} (f , \mathrm{hX} , \mathrm{hY}):= \operatorname{ndrec} (\operatorname{ndrec} (f , \mathrm{hY}), \mathrm{hX})\in \operatorname{Hom} (\mathrm{X'} , \mathrm{Y'})$
French

Mathlib.Combinatorics.Quiver.ConnectedComponent

Quiver.IsSStronglyConnecteddefMathlib.Combinatorics.Quiver.ConnectedComponent, predicate
Wording
V is strongly connected (from the name)
English
Let $V$ be a set. Then $V$ is strongly connected, if for all elements $i$ and $j$ of $V$, there exists a path $p$ of $i$ and $j$, such that $0$ is less than the length of $p$.
Formula
$V \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{IsSStronglyConnected} (V):\Leftrightarrow \forall i \in V . \forall j \in V . \exists p \in \operatorname{Path} (i , j). 0 < \operatorname{length} (p)$
French
Soit $V$ un ensemble. Alors $V$ est strongly connected si pour tous les éléments $i$ et $j$ de $V$, il existe un chemin $p$ de $i$ et $j$ tel que $0$ soit inférieur à la longueur de $p$.
Quiver.IsStronglyConnecteddefMathlib.Combinatorics.Quiver.ConnectedComponent, predicate
Wording
V is strongly connected (from the name)
English
Let $V$ be a set. Then $V$ is strongly connected, if for all elements $i$ and $j$ of $V$, $\mathrm{Nonempty}$ holds for the set of paths of $i$ and $j$.
Formula
$V \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{IsStronglyConnected} (V):\Leftrightarrow \forall i \in V . \forall j \in V . \mathrm{Nonempty} (\operatorname{Path} (i , j))$
French
Soit $V$ un ensemble. Alors $V$ est strongly connected si pour tous les éléments $i$ et $j$ de $V$, $\mathrm{Nonempty}$ tient pour l'ensemble de chemins de $i$ et $j$.

Soit $x_{1}$ un chemin de $a$ et $b$. Soit $x_{2}$ un homomorphisme de $b$ et $c$. Alors $\operatorname{cons}$ appliqué à $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$ est un chemin de $a$ et $c$.
Quiver.Path.ctorElimTypedefMathlib.Combinatorics.Quiver.Path, type
Wording
no wording
English
Let $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ be a natural number. Then an element of $\operatorname{ctorElimType}$ applied to $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ is an element of the function that maps $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ to $\mathrm{cond}$ applied to $\operatorname{ble}$ applied to $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ and $0$, $\mathrm{PULift}$ applied to $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $a$ and $\operatorname{nil}$ and $\mathrm{PULift}$ applied to the set of functions from elements $b$ of $V$ and elements $c$ of $V$ and paths $x_{1}$ of $a$ and $b$ and homomorphisms $x_{2}$ of $b$ and $c$ to elements of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $c$ and $\operatorname{cons}$ applied to $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{ctorIdx} \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{ctorElimType} (\mathrm{ctorIdx}):= \lambda \mathrm{ctorIdx} . \mathrm{cond} (\operatorname{ble} (\mathrm{ctorIdx} , 0), \mathrm{PULift} (\mathrm{motive} (a , \operatorname{nil})), \mathrm{PULift} (V \rightarrow V \rightarrow \operatorname{Path} (a , b)\rightarrow \operatorname{Hom} (b , c)\rightarrow \mathrm{motive} (c , \operatorname{cons} (x_{1} , x_{2}))))$
French
Soit $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ un nombre naturel. Alors un élément de $\operatorname{ctorElimType}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ est un élément de la fonction qui envoie $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ à $\mathrm{cond}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{ble}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{ctorIdx}$ et $0$, $\mathrm{PULift}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $a$ et $\operatorname{nil}$ et $\mathrm{PULift}$ appliqué à l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments $b$ de $V$ et des éléments $c$ de $V$ et des chemins $x_{1}$ de $a$ et $b$ et des homomorphismes $x_{2}$ de $b$ et $c$ à des éléments de $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $c$ et $\operatorname{cons}$ appliqué à $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$.

Quiver.ReachabledefMathlib.Combinatorics.Quiver.Path, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $a$ and $b$ be elements of $V$. Then $\operatorname{Reachable}$ holds for $a$ and $b$, if $\mathrm{Nonempty}$ holds for the set of paths of $a$ and $b$.
Formula
$a , b \in V \vdash \operatorname{Reachable} (a , b):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{Nonempty} (\operatorname{Path} (a , b))$
French
Soient $a$ et $b$ des éléments de $V$. Alors $\operatorname{Reachable}$ tient pour $a$ et $b$ si $\mathrm{Nonempty}$ tient pour l'ensemble de chemins de $a$ et $b$.
